As of 2023, there are 354 municipal bus lines serving over 1.75 million passengers per day, as well as intercity lines.<ref name="Bus fleet numbers within municipality">{{cite web |url=https://www.data.rio/documents/c09ef8f7d5de472c8624639070d97868/about |title=Total de linhas, frota operante, passsageiros transportados, viagens realizadas, quilometragem coberta, combustível utilizado e pessoal ocupado pelo sistema de ônibus no Município do Rio de Janeiro entre 1984-2023 |access-date=7 Mar 2025 |publisher=Data Rio |language=pt}}</ref> The city has 20 [[bus lanes]].<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.data.rio/datasets/PCRJ::brs-servi%C3%A7o-r%C3%A1pido-de-%C3%B4nibus/about |title=BRS (Serviço Rápido de Ônibus) |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=Data Rio |language=pt}}</ref> Most conventional bus routes are operated by 28 private companies divided into 4 [[consortiums]], though 3 routes are run by the city government.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://rioonibus.com/sobre-o-rio-onibus/ |title=Sobre o Rio Ônibus |date=6 January 2023 |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=Rio Ônibus |language=pt}}</ref><ref name="MOBI-Rio">{{cite web |url=https://mobi-rio.rio.br/ |title=MOBI-Rio |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=COMPANHIA MUNICIPAL DE TRANSPORTES COLETIVOS |language=pt |archive-date=29 March 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250329185721/https://mobi-rio.rio.br/ |url-status=dead }}</ref> Although most buses are [[Transit bus|conventional city buses]], there are also a handful of [[coach bus]]es used for local intracity service with more expensive fares.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://freewalkertours.com/how-to-get-from-galeao-airport-to-copacabana-learn-more/ |title=How to get from Galeão Airport to Copacabana – Learn more |date=6 December 2019 |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Free Walker Tours}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://rionoticias.com.br/glossario/frescao-rio-de-janeiro-informacoes/ |title=Frescão rio de janeiro |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Rio Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=18 April 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250418124353/https://rionoticias.com.br/glossario/frescao-rio-de-janeiro-informacoes/ }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.wikirio.com.br/Fresc%C3%A3o |title=Frescão |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Wiki Rio |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.1746.rio/hc/pt-br/articles/10870203087515-Valores-das-tarifas-de-%C3%B4nibus-e-integra%C3%A7%C3%B5es |title=Valores das tarifas de ônibus e integrações |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Rio 1746 |language=pt |archive-date=19 May 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250519211410/https://www.1746.rio/hc/pt-br/articles/10870203087515-Valores-das-tarifas-de-%C3%B4nibus-e-integra%C3%A7%C3%B5es |url-status=dead }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Costa |first=João Vitor |url=https://extra.globo.com/noticias/rio/sofrer-na-fila-ou-pegar-frescao-linha-de-onibus-no-rio-enfrenta-concorrencia-da-propria-empresa-25442062.html |date=22 Mar 2022 |title=Sofrer na fila ou pegar o frescão? Linha de ônibus no Rio enfrenta concorrência da própria empresa |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Extra |language=pt}}</ref>

The [[Bus Rapid Transit]] system consists of 31 lines running along 4 corridors with [[limited-stop|a wide variety of service patterns]]: [[TransBrasil|Transbrasil]], [[Transcarioca]], [[Transolímpica]] and [[Transoeste]]. The system is run by the municipal government through Mobi-Rio, which is a [[state-owned enterprise]].<ref name="MOBI-Rio"/> It is {{cvt|157|km|mi|0|sp=us}} long and has 153 stations as well as 731 buses that carry 535,000 passengers per day.<ref>{{cite web |last=Sena |first=Yuri |url=https://diariodotransporte.com.br/2025/03/21/prefeitura-do-rio-de-janeiro-completa-quatro-anos-na-gestao-do-sistema-brt-com-expansao-da-frota-e-aumento-de-passageiros/ |date=21 Mar 2025 |title=Prefeitura do Rio completa quatro anos na gestão do sistema BRT com expansão da frota e aumento de passageiros |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Diário do Transporte |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://cor.rio/prefeitura-do-rio-inicia-neste-sabado-30-03-a-nova-operacao-do-brt-transbrasil-e-da-avenida-brasil/ |date=28 Mar 2024 |title=Nova operação do BRT Transbrasil e da Avenida Brasil vai ocorrer diariamente das 10h às 15h |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Centro de Operações Rio |language=pt}}</ref>

In addition to buses, Rio de Janeiro also has an extensive system of [[Share taxi|vans]] used for public transport. This mode can more effectively traverse the steep, narrow and winding roads common in many [[favelas]] than buses and is often a lifeline for them.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://prefeitura.rio/transportes/prefeitura-cria-novo-codigo-disciplinar-para-transporte-complementar-cabritinhos-de-56-comunidades-do-rio/ |date=3 Mar 2023 |title=Prefeitura cria novo código disciplinar para transporte complementar conhecido como "cabritinho" de 56 comunidades do Rio |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Prefeitura da Cidade do Rio |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite act |url=https://doweb.rio.rj.gov.br/apifront/portal/edicoes/imprimir_materia/925660/5730?find=DECRETO%20RIO%20N%C2%BA%2052095%20DE%203%20DE%20MAR%C3%87O%20DE%202023 |date=3 Mar 2023 |title=Aprova o Regulamento e o Código Disciplinar do Serviço de Transporte de Passageiros Complementar Comunitário do Município do Rio de Janeiro, "Cabritinho" - STPC. |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |authority=Rio de Janeiro City Government |type=DECRETO RIO Nº 52095/2023 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Euclides |editor-last=Carvalho |first=Hélio |editor-first=Edu |url=https://mareonline.com.br/vans-e-kombis-sao-solucao-diaria-para-passageiros-que-vivem-na-mare/ |date=26 Jan 2022 |title=Vans e kombis são solução diária para passageiros que vivem na Maré |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Maré Online |language=pt}}</ref> However, most vans are run by [[Organized crime|criminal organizations]], particularly [[Brazilian police militias|police militias]], which use their fares as a major source of revenue: Of the approximately 10,000 vans used for public transport in the city, only 2,000 are legal. The 2,000 legal vans carry approximately 310,000 passengers per day.<ref>{{cite report |last=Sampaio |first=Antônio |url=https://globalinitiative.net/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/GIZ-PB-Rio-web.pdf |date=Oct 2021 |title=Criminal Governance in Rio de Janeiro |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=[[Global Initiative Against Transnational Organized Crime]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Regueira |first=Chico |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2021/08/26/vans-ilegais-sao-cerca-de-80percent-da-frota-no-rio-e-tem-ate-bancada-na-camara-de-vereadores.ghtml |date=26 Aug 2021 |title=Vans ilegais são cerca de 80% da frota no Rio e denúncias citam apoio político |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=G1 |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Regueira |first=Chico |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2021/08/24/raio-x-das-vans-no-rio-rj2.ghtml |date=24 Aug 2021 |title=Raio-x das vans no Rio: milicianos e traficantes lucram milhões à base de propinas a agentes públicos e violência |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=G1 |language=pt}}</ref>

Rio de Janeiro also has [[Intercity bus service|intercity, interstate and international bus lines]]. Most long-distance bus lines run to [[Novo Rio Bus Terminal]] at the edge of the [[Zona Central, Rio de Janeiro|Zona Central]].<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.buscaonibus.com.br/en/bus-stations/rio-de-janeiro-novo-rio |title=Rio de Janeiro Bus Station - Novo Rio |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=BuscaOnibus}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.busbrazil.com/rio-bus-terminal |title=Novo Rio Bus Terminal in Rio de Janeiro |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=Buses in Brazil}}</ref> Long-distance buses also run to [[bus stations]] in [[Campo Grande, Rio de Janeiro|Campo Grande]]<ref>{{cite web |url=https://rodoviariaonline.com.br/rodoviaria/campo-grande-rj/ |title=Rodoviária de Campo Grande RJ |access-date=1 Apr 2025 |publisher=Rodoviariaonline |language=pt}}</ref> and [[Barra da Tijuca]].<ref>{{cite web |url=https://queropassagem.com.br/rodoviaria-de-alvorada |title=Top 10 destinos partindo de Rio de Janeiro, RJ - Barra da Tijuca (Terminal Alvorada) |access-date=10 Apr 2025 |publisher=Quero Passagem |language=pt}}</ref>

====Urban rail====
{{Main|Rio de Janeiro Metro|SuperVia|Rio de Janeiro Light Rail|Santa Teresa Tram}}

{{multiple image
| perrow            = 2
| total_width       = 350
| image1            = Metro_Rio_01_2013_Ipanema_Osorio_5376.JPG
| caption1          = Train of Rio de Janeiro Metro
| image2            = Estação_Corte_8_-_Trem_Serie_3000_(2).jpg
| caption2          = Urban train from SuperVia
| image3            = VLT_na_Praça_Mauá_02.jpg

| image4            = Rio_de_Janeiro_tram_06_at_Largo_do_Guimarães.jpg
| caption4          = Santa Teresa Tram
| align             = right

Rio de Janeiro nominally has three [[Rapid transit|metro]] lines, though two of them (Lines [[Line 1 (Rio de Janeiro)|1]] and [[Line 4 (Rio de Janeiro)|4]]) are operationally run as a single line.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2024 |title=User Guide |url=https://www.metrorio.com.br/GuiaDoCliente/SuaViagem |access-date=27 Jan 2025}}</ref> The system runs on {{cvt|58|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of track and has 41 stations.<ref>{{Cite web |date=21 February 2008 |title=O DIA Online&nbsp;– Linha 3 do metrô vai receber R$35 milhões da União |trans-title=O DIA Online&nbsp;– Line 3 of the metro will receive R$35 million from the Union |website=odia.terra.com.br |url=http://odia.terra.com.br/rio/htm/linha_3_do_metro_vai_receber_r_35_milhoes_da_uniao_152725.asp |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20080226043911/http://odia.terra.com.br/rio/htm/linha_3_do_metro_vai_receber_r_35_milhoes_da_uniao_152725.asp |archive-date=26 February 2008 |access-date=6 May 2009 |publisher=[[O Dia]] |language=pt}}</ref> Line 1/4 runs from [[Uruguai Station|Uruguai/Tijuca station]] in [[Tijuca]] to [[Jardim Oceânico Station|Jardim Oceânico/Barra da Tijuca station]] in [[Barra da Tijuca]]; [[Line 2 (Rio de Janeiro)|Line 2]] runs from [[Pavuna Station|Pavuna station]] in [[Pavuna]] to [[Botafogo Station|Botafogo station]] in [[Botafogo]]. Line 1/4 and Line 2 share tracks between [[Central do Brasil]] and Botafogo. The metro system carries 651,000 [[passengers]] per day.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.data.rio/documents/0e9542bca4e34b7cbdfc636691bfcfdc/about |title=Fluxo médio de passageiros por dia útil nas linhas da rede metroviária no Município do Rio de Janeiro entre 1995-2023 |trans-title=Average flow of passengers per weekday on the metro lines in the Municipality of Rio de Janeiro between 1995-2023 |date=2023 |access-date=12 Apr 2025 |publisher=Data Rio |language=pt}}</ref>

The city also has a [[suburban rail]] system operated by [[SuperVia]] that connects the city of Rio with other locations in [[Greater Rio de Janeiro]]. It has 8 lines on {{cvt|270|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of track with 103 stations in 12 [[Brazilian municipalities|municipalities]]; Rio de Janeiro municipality itself has {{cvt|127|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of track with 59 stations on 5 lines. The [[Deodoro Line]] runs from [[Central do Brasil]] to [[Deodoro, Rio de Janeiro|Deodoro]] via [[Maracanã, Rio de Janeiro|Maracanã]], [[Méier]] and [[Madureira, Rio de Janeiro|Madureira]]; the [[Santa Cruz Line]] runs from Central do Brasil to [[Santa Cruz, Rio de Janeiro|Santa Cruz]], running parallel to the Deodoro line for its entire length and then via [[Realengo]], [[Bangu, Rio de Janeiro|Bangu]] and [[Campo Grande, Rio de Janeiro|Campo Grande]]; the [[Japeri Line]] shares tracks with the Santa Cruz Line between Central do Brasil and Deodoro before continuing on to [[Japeri]] via [[Anchieta, Rio de Janeiro|Anchieta]], [[Nilópolis]], [[Mesquita, Rio de Janeiro|Mesquita]], [[Nova Iguaçu]] and [[Queimados]]; the {{ill|Belford Roxo Line|lt=Belford Roxo|pt|Linha Belford Roxo}} and {{ill|Saracuruna Line|lt=Saracuruna|pt|Linha Saracuruna}} Lines run parallel to the Deodoro, Santa Cruz and Japeri Lines between Central do Brasil and Maracanã, with the Belford Roxo Line continuing on to [[Belford Roxo]] via Madureira, [[Pavuna]] and [[São João de Meriti]] and the Saracuruna Line continuing on to [[Duque de Caxias, Rio de Janeiro|Duque de Caxias]] via [[Bonsucesso]], [[Ramos, Rio de Janeiro|Ramos]] and [[Penha, Rio de Janeiro|Penha]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Know the stations |url=https://www.supervia.com.br/en/your-journey/know-the-stations/ |publisher=[[Supervia]] |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Operational Data |url=https://www.supervia.com.br/en/transparency/operational-data/ |publisher=[[Supervia]] |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Network Map |url=https://www.supervia.com.br/en/stations/network-map/station-map |access-date=27 Jan 2025 |publisher=SuperVia}}</ref> The suburban rail system carries around 342,000 [[passenger]]s per day.<ref>{{cite web |last=Motta |first=Mariana |title=SuperVia alcança maior média diária de passageiros dos últimos anos |trans-title=Supervia reaches highest daily average passenger count in recent years |url=https://diariodorio.com/supervia-alcanca-maior-media-diaria-de-passageiros-dos-ultimos-anos/ |date=22 December 2025 |publisher=Diário do Rio |access-date=27 December 2025 |language=pt-br}}</ref>

The Rio de Janeiro Light Rail is a modern [[light rail]] system that runs entirely within the [[Zona Central, Rio de Janeiro|Zona Central]] on {{cvt|28|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of track with 30 stations distributed along 4 heavily interlined lines: Line 1 runs from [[Santos Dumont Airport]] to [[Gentileza Intermodal Terminal]] via [[Carioca Station|Carioca]], the [[Port of Rio de Janeiro]] and [[Novo Rio Bus Terminal]]; Line 2 runs from [[Praça XV]] to [[Praia Formosa, Rio de Janeiro|Praia Formosa]] via {{ill|Praça Tiradentes|pt|Praça Tiradentes (Rio de Janeiro)}}, [[Central do Brasil]] and Novo Rio Bus Terminal; Line 3 runs from Santos Dumont Airport to Central do Brasil via Carioca and {{ill|Pequena África|pt}}; Line 4 shares track with Line 2 for almost its entire route, but stops at Gentileza Intermodal Terminal instead of Praia Formosa.<ref>{{Cite web |date=3 Apr 2024 |title=Line 4 of the VLT brings integration between Gentileza Terminal and Praça XV |work=Prefeitura da Cidade do Rio de Janeiro - prefeitura.rio |url=https://en.prefeitura.rio/cidade/linha-4-do-vlt-traz-a-integracao-entre-o-terminal-gentileza-e-a-praca-xv/ |access-date=27 Jan 2025 |author1=Felipeptmendes }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=O VLT Carioca |trans-title=The Carioca LRT |url=https://mobilidade.grupoccr.com.br/vltrio/sobre/ |access-date=27 Jan 2025|language=pt}}</ref> The trams are the first in the world to use a combination of ground-level power supply (APS) and on-board supercapacitor energy storage (SRS), in order to eliminate overhead lines along the entire route.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2019-11-06 |title=Rio de Janeiro inaugurates new light rail section |url=https://www.urban-transport-magazine.com/en/rio-de-janeiro-inaugurates-new-light-rail-section/ |access-date=2022-03-02 |website=Urban Transport Magazine |language=en-US |archive-date=3 March 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220303040952/https://www.urban-transport-magazine.com/en/rio-de-janeiro-inaugurates-new-light-rail-section/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref name="taut-2016july">"Rio's trams will run for the Olympics: Brazil's wire-free modern tramway opens, part of a major revitalisation project for Rio de Janeiro" (July 2016). ''[[Tramways & Urban Transit]]'', p. 244. UK: LRTA Publishing.</ref> The network uses 32 [[Alstom Citadis]] 402 [[low-floor tram]]s carrying 420 passengers each. They are [[bi-directional vehicle|bi-directional]], [[Air conditioning|air-conditioned]], have seven sections, and eight doors per side.<ref>{{Cite news |url=https://railway-news.com/first-alstom-citadis-tram-delivered-to-rio-de-janeiro/ |title=First Alstom Citadis Tram Delivered to Rio de Janeiro |date=2015-07-10 |work=Railway News |access-date=2017-11-04 |language=en-GB |archive-date=29 October 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211029063602/https://railway-news.com/first-alstom-citadis-tram-delivered-to-rio-de-janeiro/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref name="RGI-2016jun6">{{cite news |title=Rio de Janeiro tramway inaugurated |url=http://www.railwaygazette.com/news/news/cs-america/single-view/view/rio-de-janeiro-tramway-inaugurated.html |access-date=15 October 2016 |magazine=[[Railway Gazette International]] |date=6 June 2016 |archive-date=4 June 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190604095540/https://www.railwaygazette.com/news/news/cs-america/single-view/view/rio-de-janeiro-tramway-inaugurated.html }}</ref> The light rail system carries 101,000 passengers per day.<ref name="Transbrasil/Light Rail passenger numbers">{{cite web |last=Ribeiro |first=Geraldo |date=30 Mar 2025 |title=Após um ano da Transbrasil, passageiros do BRT elogiam rapidez na Avenida Brasil, mas motoristas enfrentam engarrafamentos nas outras pistas |trans-title=One year after Transbrasil opened, BRT passengers praise speed on Avenida Brasil, but drivers complain about traffic on other lanes |url=https://extra.globo.com/rio/noticia/2025/03/apos-um-ano-da-transbrasil-passageiros-do-brt-elogiam-rapidez-na-avenida-brasil-mas-motoristas-enfrentam-engarrafamentos-nas-outras-pistas.ghtml |access-date=24 Apr 2025 |publisher=[[Extra (newspaper)|Extra]] |language=pt}}</ref>

The Santa Teresa Tram runs from [[Carioca Station|Carioca]] to [[Santa Teresa, Rio de Janeiro|Santa Teresa]] and is the oldest operating electric [[tram]]way in [[Latin America]],<ref name="morrison-TLA">Morrison, Allen (November 2010; later updates). [http://www.tramz.com/tw/la.html "The Tramways of Latin America in 2017".] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101108133837/http://tramz.com/tw/la.html |date=8 November 2010 }} Retrieved 8 July 2020.</ref> having commenced electric operation in 1896, replacing [[horsecar|horse-drawn trams]] and extending the route.<ref name="morrison-brazil" /> At this time the gauge was altered to {{Track gauge|1100mm|lk=off}} so that the trams would fit on top of [[Carioca Aqueduct]].<ref name="morrison-brazil" /><ref>{{cite magazine |last=Dunn |first=Ian |title=Carioca Capers |url=https://www.sydneytramwaymuseum.com.au/tramway/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/313-Trolley-Wire-May-2008.pdf |date=May 2008 |magazine=Trolley Wire |publisher=[[Sydney Tramway Museum]] |number=313 |pages=3–13 |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=en}}</ref> It was designated a national historic monument in 1985.<ref name="morrison-brazil">{{Cite book |last=Morrison |first=Allen |url=http://www.tramz.com/br/tto/01.html |title=The Tramways of Brazil: A 130-Year Survey |publisher=Bonde Press |year=1989 |isbn=0-9622348-1-8 |location=New York |pages=17, 90–113 |access-date=8 July 2020 |archive-date=3 March 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090303014148/http://www.tramz.com/br/tto/01.html |url-status=live }}</ref>{{rp|111}} After a [[derailment]] occurred on 27 August 2011,<ref>[http://g1.globo.com/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2011/08/bombeiros-confirmam-cinco-mortos-em-acidente-com-bonde-no-rio.html G1: Bombeiros confirmam cinco mortos em acidente com bonde no Rio] {{webarchive |url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120220190928/http://g1.globo.com/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2011/08/bombeiros-confirmam-cinco-mortos-em-acidente-com-bonde-no-rio.html |date=20 February 2012 }}. Retrieved 9 May 2012.</ref> which left six dead, tram service was suspended to improve the system. The elderly tramcars, which dated from the 1950s,<ref name="morrison-vehicles">{{Cite web |last=Morrison |first=Allen |year=2014 |title=Santa Teresa Tramway: Vehicles, 1875–present |url=http://www.tramz.com/br/rj/cv/cv.html |access-date=22 July 2020 |archive-date=8 December 2015 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151208152656/http://www.tramz.com/br/rj/cv/cv.html |url-status=live }}</ref> were retired and replaced with newly built replicas that have the appearance of the old fleet but with new mechanical equipment and additional safety features;<ref name="taut-2013may">{{Cite news |last=<!--Staff writer(s); no by-line.--> |date=May 2013 |title=Worldwide Review [regular news section] |page=195 |work=[[Tramways & Urban Transit]] |publisher=LRTA Publishing |location=UK |issn=1460-8324}}</ref> delivery began in 2014.<ref name="ODia29Sep2014">{{Cite news |date=29 September 2014 |title=Bondinho de Santa Teresa passa por testes |language=pt |trans-title=Testing of the Santa Teresa tramway is a saga without end |work=[[O Dia]] |location=Rio de Janeiro |url=http://odia.ig.com.br/noticia/rio-de-janeiro/2014-09-29/testes-do-bonde-de-santa-teresa-viram-novela-sem-final.html |url-status=live |access-date=8 July 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141006134815/http://odia.ig.com.br/noticia/rio-de-janeiro/2014-09-29/testes-do-bonde-de-santa-teresa-viram-novela-sem-final.html |archive-date=6 October 2014}}</ref> The line's track was also rebuilt, and after some delays, about one-third of the line reopened in July 2015. More sections reopened later in stages, following repair of additional sections of track. The main section of the line was restored to its full pre-2011 length of {{cvt|6|km|mi|sigfig=1}} in January 2019.<ref name="taut-2020apr">{{Cite news |date=April 2020 |title=Santa Teresa Tramway developments |page=128 |work=[[Tramways & Urban Transit]] |publisher=Mainspring Enterprises Ltd. |issue=988 |location=UK |issn=1460-8324}}</ref> The {{cvt|1.7|km|mi}} Paula Mattos branch to Largo das Neves, which was also closed in 2011, was reopened in January 2025.<ref>{{cite news |title=Ramal Paula Mattos, do Bonde de Santa Teresa, é reinaugurado após mais de 10 anos desativado |trans-title=Paula Mattos Branch, on the Santa Teresa Tram, is reopened after more than 10 years closed |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2025/01/10/ramal-paula-mattos-do-bonde-de-santa-teresa-e-reinaugurado-apos-mais-de-10-anos-desativado.ghtml |date=10 January 2025 |publisher=[[G1 (website)|g1]] |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>  Santa Teresa residents can ride the tram for free, but ticket prices are prohibitively expensive for other [[carioca]]s and, because of this, the line is primarily used by tourists.<ref>{{cite news |last=Forte |first=Jay |title=Rio's Santa Teresa Bonde Operates with R$20 Tourist Tariff |url=https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-news/rio-travel/rios-santa-teresa-bonde-operates-with-r20-tourist-tariff/ |date=6 February 2017 |publisher=[[The Rio Times]] |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=en}}</ref> The tram gets an average of 1900 passengers per day.<ref>{{cite web |title=Bonde de Santa Teresa completa 129 anos com aumento de 30% nos embarques |trans-title=Santa Teresa Tram completes 129th birthday with 30% increase in boardings |url=https://www.rj.gov.br/noticias/bonde-de-santa-teresa-completa-129-anos-com-aumento-de-30-nos-embarques3406 |date=17 September 2025 |publisher=[[Rio de Janeiro state]] government |access-date=3 October 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>

====Ferries====
[[File:Barca_Pão_de_Açúcar_(2).jpg|thumb|Rio de Janeiro ferry]]

Rio de Janeiro has [[ferry]] routes running radially out from [[Praça Quinze de Novembro|Praça XV]] in the [[Centro, Rio de Janeiro|city center]] to {{interlanguage link|Praça Araribóia|pt}} (in [[Niterói]]'s city center), [[Charitas]] (in the South side of Niterói), [[Cocotá]] (on [[Governador Island]]) and [[Paquetá Island]].<ref>{{Cite web |title=Linhas, horários e tarifas |url=https://barcasrio.com.br/linhas-horarios-e-tarifas/ |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher=Barcas Rio |language=pt}}</ref> All four ferry routes run entirely through [[Guanabara Bay]].<ref>{{Cite web |title=Rio de Janeiro Ferry Map |url=https://riomap360.com/rio-de-janeiro-ferry-map |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher=Rio Map 360°}}</ref> The Praça XV-Praça Araribóia route is by far the busiest and carries [[commuters]] from Niterói, [[São Gonçalo, Rio de Janeiro|São Gonçalo]], [[Itaboraí]] and [[Maricá]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Graciano |first=Matheus |url=https://simsaogoncalo.com.br/barcas-duque-de-caxias-tem-mais-chances-que-sao-goncalo/ |date=15 May 2017 |title=Barcas em Duque de Caxias tem mais chances que em São Gonçalo |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher=Sim São Gonçalo |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite speech |last=Serafini |first=Flávio |url=http://alerjln1.alerj.rj.gov.br/taqalerj.nsf/8b99ca38e07826db032565300046fdf1/c347f5434fd2505f83258052007033ca?OpenDocument |date=20 Oct 2016 |title=A barca poderia e deveria ser muito melhor. |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher=[[ALERJ]] |lang=pt }}{{Dead link|date=June 2025 |bot=InternetArchiveBot |fix-attempted=yes }}</ref> Each ferry carries up to 2000 passengers,<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.quintoandar.com.br/guias/cidades/barcas-rj/ |date=14 Sep 2023 |title=Saiba como funcionam as barcas no Rio de Janeiro, um meio de transporte confortável e econômico para o dia a dia |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher={{interlanguage link|QuintoAndar|pt}} |language=pt}}</ref> with the ferry system as a whole carrying 35,000 passengers per day.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.data.rio/documents/PCRJ::passageiros-transportados-por-trajeto-no-sistema-hidrovi%C3%A1rio-no-munic%C3%ADpio-do-rio-de-janeiro-entre-1994-2024/about |title=Passageiros transportados, por trajeto, no sistema hidroviário no Município do Rio de Janeiro entre 1994-2024 |access-date=14 Apr 2025 |publisher=Data Rio |language=pt}}</ref>

====Cable car====
The [[Teleférico da Providência|Providência Cable Car]] is a {{cvt|721|meter|mile|2}} long [[Gondola lift|cable car]] line that runs from [[Central do Brasil]] to [[Gamboa, Rio de Janeiro|Gamboa]] via [[Morro da Providência|Providência Hill]], serving the city's oldest [[favela]].<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.gondolaproject.com/2023/08/03/teleferico-da-providencia-providencia-cable-car/ |date=3 Aug 2023 |title=TELEFÉRICO DA PROVIDÊNCIA (PROVIDENCIA CABLE CAR) |access-date=6 May 2025 |publisher=The Gondola Project}}</ref> The cable car first opened in 2014, was abandoned in 2017 and reopened in 2024.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://en.prefeitura.rio/ccpar/teleferico-da-providencia-tem-operacao-ampliada-e-passa-funcionar-com-capacidade-total/ |date=9 Feb 2025 |title=Providência Cable Car has expanded operations and now operates at full capacity |work=Prefeitura da Cidade do Rio de Janeiro - prefeitura.rio |access-date=6 May 2025 |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |last1=Leite |first1=Otavio }}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Augusto |first=Francini |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2024/04/07/teleferico-da-providencia-e-reinaugurado.ghtml |date=7 Apr 2024 |title=Teleférico do Morro da Providência é reinaugurado após 7 anos parado |trans-title=Providência Hill Cable Car is reinaugurated after being closed for 7 years |access-date=6 May 2025 |publisher=[[G1 (website)|g1]] |language=pt}}</ref> The line uses 16 gondolas and carries 9000 [[passengers]] per day.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://en.prefeitura.rio/desenvolvimento-economico/teleferico-da-providencia-ultrapassa-a-marca-de-9-mil-viagens-em-um-unico-dia/ |date=28 Feb 2025 |title=Providência Cable Car surpasses the mark of 9 thousand trips in a single day |access-date=6 May 2025 |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |url=https://odia.ig.com.br/rio-de-janeiro/2025/05/7049256-teleferico-da-providencia-volta-a-funcionar-depois-de-um-mes.html |date=2 May 2025 |title=Teleférico da Providência volta a funcionar depois de um mês |trans-title=Providência Cable Car reopens after a month |access-date=6 May 2025 |publisher=[[O Dia]] |language=pt}}</ref>

====Inclined elevators====
[[File:Plano Inclinado do Pavão-Pavãozinho.jpg|thumb|Pavão-Pavãozinho Inclined Elevator]]

Rio de Janeiro has 8 public [[inclined elevator]]s climbing 5 [[hills]], all of which are run by the [[Brazilian municipalities|municipal government]] through the Municipal Energy and Illumination Company (RioLuz).<ref name="Penna Inclined Elevator Reform">{{cite web |url=https://rioluz.prefeitura.rio/noticias/rioluz-realiza-reformas-no-plano-inclinado-da-igreja-nossa-senhora-da-penna/ |date=14 Sep 2023 |title=Rioluz realiza reformas no plano inclinado da Igreja Nossa Senhora da Penna |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.rio.rj.gov.br/dlstatic/10112/9387694/4250606/planilha_informacoes_institucionais.pdf.pdf |title=ESTRUTURA DAS UNIDADES ADMINISTRATIVAS RIOLUZ |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref> The Santa Marta Inclined Elevator runs up [[Favela Santa Marta|Dona Marta Hill]]<ref>{{cite conference |last=Rego Fagerlande |first=Sergio Moraes |url=https://journals.open.tudelft.nl/iphs/article/view/2753 |date=29 Oct 2018 |title=Tourism in the slums of Rio de Janeiro: An analysis of the urban impacts in informal areas caused by recent public interventions for the big sports events hosted by the city|book-title=Looking at the World History of Planning |volume=18 |number=1 |doi=10.7480/iphs.2018.1.2753 |conference=The 18th International Planning History Society Conference |location=Yokohama |publisher=International Planning History Society Proceedings |access-date=16 Apr 2025}}</ref> in two sections with 5 total stations<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.ferrolatino.ch/en/reports/brazil/ |title=Brasilien |publisher=Friends of Latin American Railways |access-date=16 Apr 2025}}</ref> and carries 5000 [[passengers]] per day.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://en.prefeitura.rio/rioluz/prefeitura-do-rio-entrega-reforma-do-primeiro-trecho-do-plano-inclinado-santa-marta/ |date=25 Apr 2023 |title=Rio City Hall delivers renovation of the first section of the Santa Marta inclined plane |work=Prefeitura da Cidade do Rio de Janeiro - prefeitura.rio |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |last1=Terra |first1=Luciano }}</ref> The Pavão-Pavãozinho Inclined Elevator runs from [[General Osório Station|General Osório/Ipanema station]] to [[Cantagalo-Pavão-Pavãozinho]] and consists of a single {{cvt|153|meter|ft|sigfig=3}} long line with 5 stations<ref>{{cite web |last=Vidon |first=Filipe |url=https://extra.globo.com/noticias/extra-extra/rioluz-finaliza-obras-de-reforma-do-plano-inclinado-do-pavao-pavaozinho-rv1-1-25495080.html |date=29 Apr 2022 |title=Rioluz finaliza obras de reforma do plano inclinado do Pavão Pavãozinho |publisher=Extra |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref> that carries 4000 passengers per day.<ref name="Inclined Elevator Passenger Numbers (2018)">{{cite |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref> The Penna Church Inclined Elevator is {{cvt|70|meters|ft|sigfig=3}} long<ref name="Penna Inclined Elevator Reform" /> and runs from [[Jacarepaguá]] to Our Lady of Penna Church at the top of Penna Hill,<ref>{{cite web |url=https://oglobo.globo.com/rio/bairros/barra/noticia/2023/09/08/plano-inclinado-da-igreja-nossa-senhora-da-penna-em-jacarepagua-passa-por-reforma.ghtml |date=8 Sep 2023 |title=Plano inclinado da |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt |archive-date=28 June 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250628230512/https://www.escaladas.com.br/local/id/766/Morro%20da%20Freguesia%20/%20Nossa%20Senhora%20da%20Penna |url-status=dead }}</ref> carrying 180 passengers per day.<ref name="Inclined Elevator Passenger Numbers (2018)" /> The Father Laércio Dias de Moura Inclined Elevator is a group of three inclined elevators<ref>{{cite web |last=O'Sullivan |first=Dermot |url=https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-news/rio-de-janeiro/rios-penha-church-is-a-neglected-jewel-worth-a-visit/ |date=26 Jul 2019 |title=Rio's Penha Church is a Neglected Jewel Worth a Visit |publisher=Rio Times |access-date=16 Apr 2025}}</ref> that connect [[Penha Station|Penha station]] to [[Vila Cruzeiro]] and the [[Basilica of Our Lady of Penha]];<ref>{{cite web |publisher=Voz das Comunidades |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref> the inclined elevators have a total length of {{cvt|426|meters|ft|sigfig=4}}<ref>{{cite web |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Merola |first=Ediane passengers per day.<ref>{{cite web |last=Jorge |first=Roberto |url=https://oglobo.globo.com/rioshow/eugenia-responde/noticia/2023/09/19/o-funicular-do-outeiro-da-gloria-ainda-funciona-colunista-indica-os-planos-inclinados-do-rio.ghtml |date=19 Sep 2023 |title='O funicular do Outeiro da Glória ainda funciona?' |publisher=O Globo |access-date=16 Apr 2025 |language=pt}}</ref><ref name="Inclined Elevator Passenger Numbers (2018)" />

===Roads===
[[File:Morro do Borel.jpg|thumb|Streetscape in Rio de Janeiro]]
[[File:Centro. Rio de Janeiro, Brazil (33).jpg|thumb|Pedestrian infrastructure in Rio de Janeiro]]
[[File:BikeRio 11 2015 Praça Mauá 706.JPG|thumb|[[Bike Rio]] rental station in [[Praça Mauá|Mauá Square]], Downtown Rio]]
Rio de Janeiro has {{cvt|601|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of [[limited-access roads]], {{cvt|731|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of [[arterial roads]], {{cvt|1245|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of [[collector roads]] and {{cvt|6678|km|mi|0|sp=us}} of [[Street|local roads]] with vehicular traffic. Despite representing 72% of roads with vehicular traffic, just 14% of [[traffic collisions]] and 14% of [[Epidemiology of motor vehicle collisions|traffic fatalities]] are on local roads; 38% of traffic fatalities happen on 20 roads, with [[Avenida Brasil (Rio de Janeiro)|Avenida Brasil]] alone accounting for 14%. Rio de Janeiro had 694 traffic deaths in 2022, equivalent to 11 deaths per 100,000 inhabitants.<ref>{{cite web |title=Taxa de mortalidade no trânsito no Município do Rio de Janeiro entre 2000-2022 |trans-title=Traffic mortality rate in the Municipality of Rio de Janeiro between 2000 and 2022 |url=https://www.data.rio/documents/874f371df3084631b41dbb9c9d31ba50/about |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>

Rio de Janeiro's vehicle fleet includes 3.2 million [[motorized vehicles]] - equivalent to 482 per 1000 inhabitants - including 2.2 million [[cars]], 500,000 [[motorcycles]], 400,000 [[trucks]] and 37,000 [[buses]] and [[minibuses]]. 37% of vehicles use exclusively [[Ethanol fuel in Brazil|E27]] fuel; 6% use E100; 4% use [[diesel fuel]], and 59% use [[Multifuel|various combinations]] of [[ethanol fuel|ethanol]], [[gasoline]] and [[Natural gas vehicle|natural gas]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Frota de veículos |trans-title=Vehicle Fleet |url=https://cidades.ibge.gov.br/brasil/rj/rio-de-janeiro/pesquisa/22/28120 |date=2024 |publisher=[[IBGE]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Total da frota de veículos segundo as principais classificações do Código de Trânsito Brasileiro no Município do Rio de Janeiro entre 1994-2024 |trans-title=Total vehicle fleet according to the main categories of the Brazilian Traffic Code in the Municipality of Rio de Janeiro between 1994 and 2024 |url=https://www.data.rio/documents/8b18e30073d34d42b85bd8fbac058dc8/about |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref> An average of 1.6 million motorized vehicles circulate per day in the city. Approximately 47% of all trips in Rio use public transport; 23% use private motorized vehicles and 28% use [[active mobility]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Deep Dive City Rio de Janeiro, Brazil |url=https://transformative-mobility.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Rio-de-Janeiro-Deep-Dive.pdf |publisher=Transformative Urban Mobility Initiative |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite report |title=Plano de Mobilidade Urbana Sustentável |trans-title=Sustainable Urban Mobility Plan |url=https://www.rio.rj.gov.br/documents/5450795/7289937/P7+-+Revisao+Relatorio+Sintese+20161221+rev.pdf |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>

Despite being in the [[Atlantic Forest]], Rio de Janeiro has a severe lack of [[Urban forestry|street trees]], and the distribution of the trees that do exist is highly unequal: poorer neighbourhoods in the North side of the city are disproportionately likely to lack tree cover, as are neighbourhoods with higher populations of [[Pardo Brazilians|pardo]] and [[Afro-Brazilians|black]] people. Overall, 35% of [[cariocas]] live on streets without any trees at all, and another 26% live on streets with fewer than 5 trees. This [[environmental racism]] directly causes poorer neighbourhoods to suffer from more extreme weather, including more [[Urban flooding|severe flooding]] and [[Urban heat island|higher temperatures]] - for example, [[Lagoa, Rio de Janeiro|Lagoa]] has a mean income per capita nearly 10 times higher than that of [[Del Castilho]] and has a mean temperature of {{cvt|26|C}}, as opposed to Del Castilho's {{cvt|33|C}}.<ref>{{cite journal |last1=Paes da Silva Sales |last2=Rajão |last3=Ribeiro |last4=Sartori |first1=Gabriel |first2=Henrique |first3=Fernando Patrício |first4=Richieri Antonio |title=Who has the Right to Urban Green Areas? Environmental Justice in a Brazilian Metropolis  |url=https://www.halacsolcha.org/index.php/halac/article/view/773 |doi=10.32991/2237-2717.2024v14i2.p295-325 |date=28 June 2024 |journal=Historia Ambiental Latinoamericana y Caribeña (HALAC) Revista de la Solcha |volume=14 |number=2 |pages=295–325 |access-date=26 July 2025}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Trigueiro |first=André |title=Rio tem déficit de 1 milhão de árvores, diz levantamento; saiba quais são os bairros mais arborizados |trans-title=Rio has a deficit of 1 million trees, says survey; know which neighbourhoods are the most tree-covered |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2023/09/21/rio-tem-deficit-de-1-milhao-de-arvores-diz-levantamento-saiba-quais-sao-os-bairros-mais-arborizados.ghtml |date=21 September 2023 |publisher=[[G1 (website)|g1]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last1=Trigueiro |last2=Soares |first1=André |first2=Lucas |title=Prefeitura não informa onde planta mudas que compensam remoção; Rio perdeu 35 árvores por dia em 2024 |trans-title=City government doesn't inform where it plants saplings to compensate for removals; Rio lost 35 trees per day in 2024 |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2025/06/07/prefeitura-nao-informa-onde-planta-mudas-que-compensam-remocao-rio-perdeu-35-arvores-por-dia-em-2024.ghtml |date=7 June 2025 |publisher=[[G1 (website)|g1]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>

Despite Rio de Janeiro's ample [[sidewalk]] coverage, many sidewalks are narrow, inaccessible for [[disabled people]], and filled with obstacles. [[Traffic lights]] frequently prioritize vehicular traffic on busy roads significantly more than pedestrian traffic, which can significantly increase pedestrian travel times. [[Portuguese pavement]], which is common in Rio de Janeiro,<ref>{{cite news |last=Virgilio |first=Paulo |title=Rio sidewalks, urban tattoos |url=https://agenciabrasil.ebc.com.br/en/cultura/noticia/2015-06/rio-sidewalks-urban-tattoos |date=12 June 2015 |publisher=[[Agência Brasil]] |access-date=26 July 2025}}</ref> is extremely slippery in the rain and gets easily damaged by tree roots, which further harms pedestrian accessibility. The city has [[pedestrian zones]] in the [[Centro, Rio de Janeiro|city center]], [[Paquetá Island]] and concentrated around the [[Supervia|suburban rail]] stations in [[Bangu, Rio de Janeiro|Bangu]], [[Campo Grande, Rio de Janeiro|Campo Grande]] and [[Penha, Rio de Janeiro|Penha]], as well as in [[favelas]]. Part of the city has a wayfinding system similar to [[Legible London]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Walk Rio |url=https://www.appliedinformation.group/projects/walk-rio |publisher=Applied Information Group |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Feliciano |first=Dorah |title=Paquetá: A Safe, Beautiful, Car-free Neighborhood in Rio |url=https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-news/rio-de-janeiro/travel-rio/paqueta-a-safe-beautiful-car-free-neighborhood-in-rio/ |date=28 November 2019 |publisher=[[The Rio Times]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite report |title=Plano de Segurança Viária |trans-title=Traffic Safety Plan |url=https://cetrio.prefeitura.rio/wp-content/uploads/sites/36/2023/09/Plano-de-Seguranca-Viaria_compressed-1.pdf |date=22 May 2023 |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite report |last1=Koch |last2=Lindau |last3=Nassi |first1=Jacob |first2=Luis Antonio |first3=Carlos David |title=Transportation in the Favelas of Rio de Janeiro |url=https://www.lincolninst.edu/app/uploads/legacy-files/pubfiles/koch_wp13jk1.pdf |date=2013 |publisher=[[Lincoln Institute of Land Policy]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=en}}</ref>

Rio de Janeiro has approximately 247 km of [[shared-use path]]s, 119 km of [[sharrows]], 64 km of segregated [[cycle track]]s, and 28 km of roadside [[bike lanes]]. 16% of [[cariocas]] live within 300 meters of [[bicycle infrastructure]]; the bicycle infrastructure network also reaches 30% of jobs, 16% of schools and 21% of [[health facilities]]. 127 of the city's 288 medium- and high-capacity public transport ([[Bus Rapid Transit|BRT]], [[ferry]], [[light rail]], [[Rapid Transit|metro]] and [[suburban rail]]) stations are served by bicycle infrastructure. Bicycle infrastructure in the city is frequently narrow, with 66% of it failing to reach the city government's minimum standards. The city also has a [[bicycle sharing system]] called [[Bike Rio]] with 430 stations and 6700 bicycles.<ref>{{cite web |title=Aluguel de bicicleta Rio de Janeiro |date=26 May 2021 |trans-title=Bicycle rental in Rio de Janeiro |url=https://bikeitau.com.br/rio/ |publisher=[[Bike Rio]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Alves |first=Raoni |title=Com 400 km de malha cicloviária e meta de bater mil km até 2033, Rio aumentou estrutura em 1,19% no último ano, diz estudo |trans-title=With a 400 km cycling network and a goal to reach 1000 km by 2023, Rio increased its network by 1.19% in the last year, says study |url=https://g1.globo.com/rj/rio-de-janeiro/noticia/2025/04/19/com-400-km-de-malha-cicloviaria-e-meta-de-bater-mil-km-ate-2033-rio-aumentou-estrutura-em-119percent-no-ultimo-ano-diz-estudo.ghtml |date=19 April 2025 |publisher=[[G1 (website)|g1]] |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite report |title=Plano de Expansão Cicloviária |trans-title=Cycling Expansion Plan |url=https://transportes.prefeitura.rio/wp-content/uploads/sites/31/2022/07/CicloRio-Plano-de-Expansa%CC%83o-Ciclovia%CC%81ria-transportes.pdf |publisher=Rio de Janeiro City Government |access-date=26 July 2025 |lang=pt}}</ref>

====Major highways====

[[Avenida Brasil (Rio de Janeiro)|Avenida Brasil]] (''Brazil Avenue'') is the longest and busiest [[Limited-access road|highway]] in Rio de Janeiro, stretching {{cvt|58|km|mi|0|sp=us}} from the [[Port of Rio de Janeiro|Port area]] in the [[Zona Central (Rio de Janeiro)|city center]] through the North side to [[Santa Cruz, Rio de Janeiro|Santa Cruz]] in the West side and carrying 800,000 vehicles per day.<ref>{{cite news |last=Carneiro |first=Júlia |date=19 Oct 2012 |title=Groundbreaking soap opera grips Brazil |url=https://www.bbc.com/news/world-latin-america-20001198 |access-date=22 Apr 2025 |isbn=978-65-87016-26-9 |access-date=22 Apr 2025 |publisher=Observatório de Favelas |lang=pt}}</ref> Almost the entire highway is part of [[BR-101]],<ref>{{cite web |title=Avenida Brasil towards [[Vitória, Espírito Santo|Vitória]],<ref>{{cite web |title=Business Units |work=EcoRodovias |url=https://www.ecorodovias.com.br/en/ecorodovias_en/business-units/ |access-date=25 Apr 2025
